## Note to Accredited Entities for the use of the funding proposal template

The following are key elements that should be included in the funding proposal:

- reference to issues such as feasibility studies, gender action plan, term sheet, etc.
- a clear description of the project/programme and its expected results is presented in the funding proposal. Updates to the funding proposal and/or annexes must be submitted to the GEF Secretariat by email at least five working days before the submission deadline.
- The total number of pages for the funding proposal (including annexes) should not exceed 30 pages, excluding the cover page and table of contents. The template is designed to fit within this limit.
- Please use Arial font size 11.
- Under the [Underline] section, additional information, input and programme funding proposals must be submitted in English only. Any information that may not be disclosed pursuant to the GEF Information Disclosure Policy should be clearly indicated by the applicant.
- Please submit the completed proposal for evaluation to the GEF Secretariat at least five working days before the deadline.

# A PROJECT/PROGRAMME SUMMARY

## A.1 Project or Programme Title
Green Climate Fund Proposal

## A.2 Request for Proposals (RfP)

## A.3 Project or Programme Description
The project aims to enhance climate resilience and sustainable development in the region by implementing various initiatives.

### A.3.1 Project Components
1. **Climate change adaptation:**
   - Enhancing early warning systems.
   - Improving disaster risk management.
2. **Renewable energy and power generation:**
   - Promoting solar, wind, and hydro projects.
   - Supporting the development of renewable energy infrastructure.
3. **Capacity building:**
   - Training local communities in climate resilience strategies.
   - Providing technical assistance to local governments.

### A.3.2 Project Objectives
1. Reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 35%.
2. Increase access to clean energy for rural and urban populations.
3. Enhance the adaptive capacity of vulnerable communities.

## A.4 Result Areas
1. **Climate change adaptation:**
   - Enhanced early warning systems.
   - Improved disaster risk management.
2. **Renewable energy:**
   - Increased renewable energy generation.
   - Reduced reliance on fossil fuels.
3. **Capacity building:**
   - Trained local communities and governments.

## A.3 Executive Entity

The Executing Entity is the Kingdom of Thailand, supervised by the Ministry of Agriculture and Cooperatives (MOAC).

### A.31 Project summary (max 750 words, approximately 1.5 pages)

1. **Background:** The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions from rice farming in the central region of Thailand through the adoption of sustainable agricultural practices. This includes the promotion of integrated water management and crop residue utilization.

2. **Objectives:**
   - Reduce methane emissions by 30%.
   - Increase rice yield by 15%.

3. **Implementation:** The project will be implemented in collaboration with local farmers, agricultural extension services, and non-governmental organizations (NGOs). Training programs will be conducted to educate farmers on sustainable farming practices.

4. **Budget:** The total budget for the project is USD 2 million over a period of three years. This includes costs associated with training, equipment, and monitoring activities.

5. **Expected Results:**
   - A reduction in methane emissions by 30%.
   - An increase in rice yield by 15%.

6. **Monitoring and Evaluation:** The project will be monitored through regular reports and on-site visits to ensure compliance with the project objectives. Progress will be evaluated annually.

## Project Details

- **Project Title:** Sustainable Rice Farming Initiative
- **Location:** Central Region, Thailand
- **Duration:** 3 years (2019-2021)
- **Funding Agency:** Green Climate Fund
- **Executing Entity:** Ministry of Agriculture and Cooperatives (MOAC)

### Key Milestones:
- **June 2019:** Project initiation and stakeholder meetings.
- **July 2019:** Development of project plan and budget.
- **August 2019:** Training programs for farmers begin.
- **December 2019:** Initial monitoring and evaluation activities.

### Contact Information:
- **Project Manager:** John Doe
- **Email:** john.doe@moac.gov
- **Phone:** +66 1234 5678

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v2.2 | PAGE 3 OF 8

## A

To meet even modest projections requires an acceleration of adoption in Thailand's agriculture industry during the next decade.

1. Climate impacts on agriculture will particularly affect poor households. Poorly managed Thaischakhaa systems are vulnerable to saltwater intrusion, which threatens aquaculture in the agricultural sector, such as shrimp farming and rice production. The impact of climate change on agriculture is expected to be significant, with a potential loss of up to 10% of GDP by 2050 (World Bank, 2013). Climate change will also affect urban areas, particularly those in coastal regions.

2. With climate extremes expected to increase, climate-informed water management and climate-resilient water infrastructure are critical for the sustainability of Thailand's agriculture sector. The Thai government has committed to reducing greenhouse gas emissions by 20-25% by 2030 (compared to business-as-usual) through a combination of domestic efforts and international support.

### Objectives

- Enhance climate and water-informed planning in the water and agricultural sectors through:
  - Improved data collection, analysis, and modeling.
  - Enhanced decision-making tools and infrastructure, complemented by EIA.
- Improve water management practices to enhance water use efficiency and food production areas through integrated water resource management.

### Project Summary

The project will directly benefit 20,000 people in the project site area, with projected benefits reaching 41,183 people. The project will support the development of climate-informed water management plans for the Thaischakhaa system and will help to lift Thailand out of poverty by increasing agricultural productivity and improving food security. The project will also contribute to the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), particularly SDG 1 (No Poverty) and SDG 2 (Zero Hunger).

### Cooperative Agreements

FAO will directly support the BAAF through a Memorandum of Understanding with the Ministry of Agriculture and Cooperatives. This agreement will enable FAO to enhance the agricultural sector's adaptation planning, as well as support climate-smart agriculture practices. The project will be implemented in cooperation with the Department of Agricultural Extension (DOAE), which is responsible for the development and implementation of climate change adaptation strategies.

### Project Implementation

The project will be implemented over a period of five years (2017-2022) and will focus on the following key activities:

- Conduct a comprehensive assessment of water-related challenges and opportunities in the Thaischakhaa system.
- Develop and implement climate-informed water management plans for the Thaischakhaa system.
- Provide training and capacity building to support the implementation of these plans.

### Project Budget

The total project budget is $10 million, with $5 million provided by the Green Climate Fund and $5 million provided by the Thai government. The project will be implemented in close collaboration with the Department of Agricultural Extension (DOAE) and other relevant stakeholders.

### Project Duration

The project will run from 2017 to 2022, with a total duration of five years.

# B. PROJECT/PROGRAMME INFORMATION

## B.1 Climate change and coastal defence (1000 words, approximately 2 pages)

- Pressure from climate change includes increased frequency of potential extreme events such as storm surges and sea level rise. The Environment Agency has identified that the risk of flooding will increase over time due to rising sea levels and changes in wave climate. This is expected to result in an increase in the number of properties at risk of coastal flooding.

- The Environment Agency has developed a Shoreline Management Plan (SMP) for the English Channel, Irish Sea and North Sea coastlines. The SMP identifies areas of the coastline that are likely to be affected by coastal processes over the next 100 years. The plan also includes an assessment of the risk of flooding from the sea.

- The vulnerability of the Yarmouth town centre has been identified, and the risks associated with it have been assessed in existing studies. The SMP identifies the areas at highest risk of flooding due to climate change. The SMP also provides guidance on how to manage these risks through the use of flood defences and other measures.

## B.2 Climate change impacts and research

- Increased temperatures and rainfall variability are expected over the next few decades, which will in turn increase the frequency and severity of extreme weather events such as storms and floods. This is expected to have a significant impact on coastal communities and infrastructure.

- The Environment Agency has developed guidance for local authorities on how to manage the risks associated with climate change. The guidance includes advice on how to assess the risk of flooding from the sea, how to develop flood defence strategies, and how to implement these strategies in practice.

## B.3 Limited A and Trustee B (2016)

- Limited A and Trustee B are involved in the development and implementation of climate change adaptation measures in Yarmouth. They have been working with the Environment Agency to assess the risks associated with coastal flooding and to develop strategies for managing these risks.

- The company has also been involved in the development of a Climate Change Risk Assessment (CCRA) for Yarmouth, which identifies the key risks associated with climate change and provides recommendations for how to manage these risks.

## Figure 1: Maximum, mean and minimum temperatures

Nationwide averages of rapid air temperature change in annual totals since 1900 (see Annex 2, section 17). The figure shows the average annual maximum, mean and minimum temperatures over the period. The data is sourced from the National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA) and the Climate Prediction Center (CPC), which provide long-term climate data for the United States. The graph indicates a significant increase in temperature over the past century, with notable rises in both maximum and minimum temperatures. This trend is consistent with global warming patterns observed since the late 1970s.

## Figure 2: Average Daily Dry Season Precipitation

Average daily dry season precipitation (2005-2014) for the northern and central areas of the Chaco-Pampa region (see Figure 2, page 8). The data is sourced from the National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA) and the Climate Prediction Center (CPC), which provide long-term climate data for the United States. The graph shows a significant decrease in precipitation over the past decade, with notable reductions in both maximum and minimum precipitation levels. This trend is consistent with global warming patterns observed since the late 1970s.

## Experiences evaluated through qualitative analysis

### 1.1. Overview of the ECHAM5 GCM and PRECIS climate models

The ECHAM5 GCM (Global Climate Model) and PRECIS (Providing Regional Climates for Impact Studies) are two widely used climate models that provide regional climate projections over the whole country. These models have been extensively validated against observed data to ensure their accuracy.

### 1.2. Comparison of model outputs with observed data

The outputs from the ECHAM5 GCM and PRECIS models were compared with observed data from various sources, including the National Centers for Environmental Information (NCEI) and the Global Historical Climatology Network (GHCN). The comparison showed a high degree of agreement between the model outputs and the observed data.

### 1.3. Analysis of future climate projections

The ECHAM5 GCM and PRECIS models were used to simulate future climate scenarios for the Chaco-Pampa region. These simulations indicate that the region is likely to experience significant changes in temperature and precipitation patterns over the next century, with potential impacts on water resources, agriculture, and biodiversity.

### B

18. BC already provides other adaptation measures in the coastal areas such as beach nourishment, sand filling, and raising water levels during high tides. These measures have been shown to be effective in reducing flooding risks along the coast. The government has also implemented a number of measures to reduce the impact of climate change on agriculture, including:

- Provision of early warning systems for floods and droughts.
- Establishment of irrigation schemes to improve water availability during dry seasons.
- Promotion of climate-smart agriculture practices.

19. The government is also implementing other adaptation measures, particularly the proposed project under the GCF, which aims to build in the following areas:

- Improved water management infrastructure.
- Increased access to clean water and sanitation facilities.
- Improved agricultural productivity through improved irrigation systems.

20. The KRI shows that water management practices (i) need prediction, particularly given the risks of increased variability in rainfall patterns; and (ii) adequate water storage through irrigation schemes, which are needed on agriculture and agriculture outputs.

IWRM is a process that involves different stakeholders working together to make decisions that will improve water management. The process is a key component of the National Water Resources Management Plan. It is a tool for monitoring progress towards achieving the objectives set out in the National Water Resources Management Plan.

# Key Characteristics of Thailand's Agricultural Industry

## Agriculture 1.0
### Traditional Farming
- **Characteristics**: Small-scale farming, low productivity.
- **Examples of Crops**: Rice, corn, cassava.

## Agriculture 2.0
### Use of Technology
- **Characteristics**: Increased use of technology, higher yields.
- **Examples of Crops**: Rice, sugarcane, fruits.

## Agriculture 3.0
### Smart Farming
- **Characteristics**: High-tech farming, precision agriculture.
- **Examples of Crops**: Rice, vegetables, livestock.

## Water Management in Chao Phraya River Basin

Water management challenges in the Chao Phraya River Basin include:
- Flood and drought risks.
- RIO developed a 20-year strategic water management plan focusing on achieving water security and sustainable development services with 2036 as the ultimate goal.
- The plan aims to balance water demand among various users, including farmers, industries, and urban areas.

### Existing Initiatives
- **Water Management**: Efforts in the proposed project area (Yom and Nan River Basin) include:
  - **Water Quality Improvement**: Reducing pollution from agricultural runoff.
  - **Water Conservation**: Implementing efficient irrigation practices to reduce water waste.
  - **Flood Control**: Improving flood management systems.

### Existing Initiatives
- **Water Management**: Efforts in the proposed project area (Yom and Nan River Basin) include:
  - **Water Quality Improvement**: Reducing pollution from agricultural runoff.
  - **Water Conservation**: Implementing efficient irrigation practices to reduce water waste.
  - **Flood Control**: Improving flood management systems.

### The Proposed Plan
- **Water Management**: The plan includes provisions for Phrae, Sukhothai, and Uttaradit in a large-scale integrated river basin management approach.
- **Stakeholder Engagement**: Involves local communities, government agencies, and private sector participation to ensure sustainable water use.

### RIO's Goals
- **Water Security**: Achieving long-term solutions for the challenges presented by climate change, increased urbanization, and population growth.
- **Sustainable Development**: Ensuring effective water management practices that support economic development while preserving natural resources.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## PAGE 1 OF 8

### 30

To avoid sudden environmental impacts to the natural Chief Phnohs River system, RDC recommends a timely implementation of an improved dam safety system. This includes:

- Regular inspection and maintenance of existing dams.
- Upgrading of existing systems to current standards.
- Implementation of a dam safety program.

The project will include the following components:
- Assessment of existing dams.
- Development of a dam safety program.
- Training for local staff on dam safety procedures.
- Acquisition of equipment and materials required for inspections and maintenance.
- Establishment of a database to track inspections, repairs, and upgrades.
- Implementation of an early warning system.

The project will be implemented through the following steps:
1. Conduct a comprehensive assessment of existing dams.
2. Develop a dam safety program based on the assessment.
3. Train local staff on dam safety procedures.
4. Acquire equipment and materials required for inspections and maintenance.
5. Establish a database to track inspections, repairs, and upgrades.
6. Implement an early warning system.

### 31

The project will be implemented in two phases:
- Phase I: Assessment of existing dams and development of the dam safety program (1 year).
- Phase II: Implementation of the dam safety program, including training, equipment acquisition, database establishment, and early warning system implementation (2 years).

The total cost of the project is estimated at USD 500,000. This includes:
- Assessment costs: USD 100,000.
- Program development costs: USD 50,000.
- Training costs: USD 75,000.
- Equipment and materials costs: USD 200,000.
- Database establishment costs: USD 50,000.
- Early warning system implementation costs: USD 125,000.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## B

Global trends indicate wide and rapid adoption of innovative agricultural and water saving technologies for crop production. These technologies are increasingly being adopted in developing countries to improve food security. The use of these technologies is expected to increase further as a result of water scarcity, land degradation, and climate change. The need to adopt new technologies is particularly important in the face of increasing population pressure on limited natural resources.

### 3.1 Theory of Change

The theory of change focuses on good design, capacity building, and empowerment to enhance the resilience of smallholder farmers and local communities in the face of climate change. This approach will be achieved through the following activities:

- **Capacity Building:** Provide training and technical assistance to smallholder farmers.
- **Empowerment:** Empower local communities to take charge of their own development.
- **Knowledge Sharing:** Facilitate knowledge sharing among farmers, extension workers, and other stakeholders.

### 3.2 List of Activities

The following activities will be undertaken to achieve the objectives outlined above:

1. **Capacity Building:**
   - Conduct training sessions on climate-smart agriculture practices.
   - Provide access to credit for smallholder farmers.
   - Develop and distribute information materials on climate change adaptation strategies.

2. **Empowerment:**
   - Establish farmer field schools to promote participatory learning.
   - Facilitate the formation of farmer cooperatives.
   - Provide access to markets for smallholder farmers.

3. **Knowledge Sharing:**
   - Organize workshops and seminars to share best practices.
   - Develop a website and mobile application to facilitate knowledge sharing.
   - Establish partnerships with research institutions to promote innovation.

### 3.3 Results Framework

The results framework will be used to monitor and evaluate the progress of the project. The following indicators will be tracked:

- **Output Indicators:**
  - Number of training sessions conducted.
  - Number of farmers trained.
  - Number of farmer cooperatives established.

- **Outcome Indicators:**
  - Increase in agricultural productivity.
  - Increase in income levels of smallholder farmers.
  - Improvement in food security at the household level.

### 3.4 Innovative Engineering Designs

Innovative engineering designs will be used to enhance water efficiency, reduce erosion, and improve soil health. This includes:

- **Water Harvesting:**
  - Construction of small dams and reservoirs.
  - Installation of rainwater harvesting systems.

- **Soil Conservation:**
  - Implementation of contour farming.
  - Establishment of windbreaks.

### 3.5 Ecosystem-Based Adaptation (EBA)

EBA measures are cost-effective for smallholder farmers and can be implemented with minimal external support. Examples include:

- **Agroforestry:**
  - Planting trees on farms to improve soil health and provide shade.
  - Establishment of fruit orchards.

- **Wetlands:**
  - Restoration of wetlands to improve water storage and filtration.
  - Promotion of sustainable irrigation practices.

### 3.6 Livelihood Support

Support will be provided to smallholder farmers to enhance their livelihoods through:

- **Diversification:**
  - Promotion of crop diversification.
  - Establishment of value chains for new crops.

- **Market Access:**
  - Facilitation of access to markets.
  - Provision of business development services.

### 3.7 Gender and Social Inclusion

The project will ensure that women and marginalized groups have equal access to resources and opportunities. This includes:

- **Training:**
  - Conducting gender-sensitive training sessions.
  - Providing targeted support to women farmers.

- **Access to Resources:**
  - Ensuring equal access to credit and other financial services.
  - Promoting the use of appropriate technologies for women.

### 3.8 Monitoring and Evaluation

A comprehensive monitoring and evaluation system will be established to track progress towards achieving the project objectives. This includes:

- **Baseline Surveys:**
  - Conducting baseline surveys to assess initial conditions.
  - Establishing a system for regular data collection.

- **Reporting:**
  - Preparation of quarterly progress reports.
  - Annual evaluation of project performance.

### 3.9 Financial Management

The financial management plan outlines the budget and financial arrangements for the implementation of the project. This includes:

- **Budget Allocation:**
  - Detailed breakdown of costs by activity.
  - Allocation of funds to implementing partners.

- **Financial Reporting:**
  - Preparation of monthly financial reports.
  - Annual audits to ensure accountability.

### 3.10 Risk Management

The risk management plan identifies potential risks and outlines strategies to mitigate them. This includes:

- **Risk Identification:**
  - Identification of potential risks.
  - Assessment of the likelihood and impact of each risk.

- **Mitigation Strategies:**
  - Development of contingency plans.
  - Regular review and update of the risk management plan.

### 3.11 Communication and Advocacy

A communication and advocacy strategy will be developed to raise awareness about the project and its achievements. This includes:

- **Awareness Raising:**
  - Organization of public events and workshops.
  - Distribution of information materials.

- **Partnerships:**
  - Establishment of partnerships with media outlets.
  - Collaboration with civil society organizations.

### 3.12 Capacity Building

Capacity building will be a key component of the project to ensure sustainability beyond the life of the project. This includes:

- **Training:**
  - Conducting training sessions for implementing partners.
  - Providing on-the-job training and mentoring.

- **Institutional Strengthening:**
  - Support to local institutions to enhance their capacity.
  - Promotion of South-South cooperation.

### 3.13 Knowledge Management

A knowledge management system will be established to capture, store, and share lessons learned throughout the project lifecycle. This includes:

- **Documentation:**

  - Publication of project reports and articles.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal (v2.1) | PAGE 14 OF 83

## Enhancing climate resilience is achieved through effective disaster risk management and adaptation planning.

### Project Objectives

- **Objective 1:** Strengthen national and local systems for effective implementation of the Sendai Framework for Disaster Risk Reduction.
- **Objective 2:** Enhance community-based disaster risk management (CBDRM) to reduce vulnerability and enhance resilience at the grassroots level.
- **Objective 3:** Improve climate change adaptation planning and implementation.

### Project Components

1. **Component 1: Institutional Strengthening**
   - Sub-component 1.1: Policy and regulatory framework
   - Sub-component 1.2: Capacity building for disaster risk management (DRM)
   - Sub-component 1.3: Coordination mechanisms

2. **Component 2: Community-Based Disaster Risk Management**
   - Sub-component 2.1: Community-based early warning systems
   - Sub-component 2.2: Community-based disaster preparedness and response
   - Sub-component 2.3: Community-based risk reduction initiatives

3. **Component 3: Adaptation Planning and Implementation**
   - Sub-component 3.1: Climate change impact assessment
   - Sub-component 3.2: Development of climate change adaptation plans
   - Sub-component 3.3: Implementation of adaptation measures

### Theory of Change

![Figure 5: Theory of Change](image_url)

### Project Objectives, Outcomes, and Impacts

The target communities for the proposed project are in three provinces of the southern region of Thailand. The project will be implemented through a participatory approach with local governments and communities.

- **Outcome 1:** Enhanced institutional capacity to manage disaster risk at national and sub-national levels.
- **Outcome 2:** Strengthened community-based disaster risk management (CBDRM) systems.
- **Outcome 3:** Improved climate change adaptation planning and implementation.

### Results Framework

The project will be implemented through a results-based approach. The expected outcomes are:

1. **Increased capacity of national and sub-national institutions to manage disaster risk.**
2. **Enhanced community-based disaster risk management (CBDRM) systems.**
3. **Improved climate change adaptation planning and implementation.**

### Project Implementation

The project will be implemented over a period of five years, with an initial focus on the most vulnerable communities. The project will be implemented through a participatory approach, involving local governments, communities, and other stakeholders.

### Monitoring and Evaluation

The project will include a comprehensive monitoring and evaluation (M&E) system to track progress towards the expected outcomes. The M&E system will include indicators for each outcome, as well as a results framework to track progress over time.

**Figure B: Preferential map of Thailand**

The major area designated for the project is in the larger Chao Phraya river basin. The project will be implemented in three phases over a period of ten years.

### 61

These processes were selected by RDO under MOU/Consultation with the Office of Natural Resources and Environmental Conservation (ONREC) and the Department of Water Resources (DWR), Ministry of Natural Resources and Environment, Thailand. The project area is situated in the lower part of the Chao Phraya river basin, which is one of the most important agricultural areas in the country. It has a water retention function, saving a flood of water bodies (gaps) to the country's strategic plan, and ultimately promoting sustainable development.

### 62

With co-financing and GCF financing, the project aims to support climate-informed water management, planning, and decision-making processes in the lower Chao Phraya river basin. The project will enhance climate resilience and improve adaptive capacity as well as reduce vulnerability to climate change impacts through improved water resource management and integrated water resources management (IWRM) approaches.

- **Output 1: Enhanced climate and water information services** through improved climate information and early warning systems.
- **Output 2: Improved water resource management** through enhanced water resource monitoring, planning, and decision-making processes.
- **Output 3: Improved water resource management** through enhanced water resource monitoring, planning, and decision-making processes.

### 63

The project will complement ongoing efforts from RIT to enhance the resilience of the country and to address climate change impacts. The project will also support the implementation of the National Water Resources Management Master Plan (2017-2036) in the lower part of the Chao Phraya river basin, which is one of the most important agricultural areas in the country.

### 64

The project will be implemented over a period of ten years. The project will be implemented through a series of activities that are designed to enhance climate resilience and improve adaptive capacity as well as reduce vulnerability to climate change impacts.

## PAGE 1 OF 8

Through the adoption of resource-efficient technologies and practices (e.g., using drought-tolerant crops and irrigation of water-scarce areas) to increase agricultural productivity.

### Response and methodologies for targeting the project areas and populations

The project will target smallholder farmers in the drylands, particularly those who are vulnerable to climate change impacts. The project will focus on women and youth, who are often excluded from decision-making processes and have limited access to resources. The project will be implemented in collaboration with local governments, non-governmental organizations (NGOs), and other stakeholders.

### Output 1: Enhance climate risk informed planning in the water and agricultural sectors through improved climate information and tools

#### Activity 1.1: Develop and disseminate climate information products and services

GCF resources will support the improvement of climate information decision making in Thailand through the development of climate information products and services. The project will develop a web-based platform to provide climate information, including seasonal forecasts, early warnings, and climate risk assessments. The project will also develop a mobile application to provide climate information to farmers.

### Output 2: Enhance the capacity of smallholder farmers to generate income from water and agricultural management

#### Activity 2.1: Develop and implement water and agricultural management practices

This activity aims to strengthen the climate resilience of smallholder farmers by improving their access to water and agricultural inputs. The project will provide training on water management, including rainwater harvesting, irrigation systems, and soil conservation techniques. The project will also provide training on agricultural practices, including crop diversification, integrated pest management, and post-harvest handling.

#### Activity 2.2: Develop and implement climate-smart agriculture practices

This activity aims to enhance the resilience of smallholder farmers by promoting climate-smart agriculture practices. The project will provide training on climate-smart agriculture practices, including drought-tolerant crops, crop diversification, and integrated pest management. The project will also provide training on climate risk management, including early warning systems, insurance, and index-based livestock insurance.

### Output 3: Enhance the capacity of smallholder farmers to generate income from water and agricultural management

#### Activity 3.1: Develop and implement water and agricultural management practices

This activity aims to strengthen the climate resilience of smallholder farmers by improving their access to water and agricultural inputs. The project will provide training on water management, including rainwater harvesting, irrigation systems, and soil conservation techniques. The project will also provide training on agricultural practices, including crop diversification, integrated pest management, and post-harvest handling.

#### Activity 3.2: Develop and implement climate-smart agriculture practices

The project will provide training on climate-smart agriculture practices, including drought-tolerant crops, crop diversification, and integrated pest management. ### Output 4: Sub-national activities

#### Activity 4.1: Develop and implement sub-national activities

This activity aims to enhance the capacity of local governments to implement climate change adaptation measures. The project will provide training on climate risk management, including early warning systems, insurance, and index-based livestock insurance. The project will also provide technical assistance to local governments to develop and implement climate change adaptation plans.

### Submitted to:

### Objectives:

#### 7.1
- **Sub-objective:**
  - Increase the number of provinces and districts that have access to clean water and sanitation.
    - **Target:**
      - 50 provinces and districts by 2023.
    - **Indicators:**
      - Number of provinces and districts with access to clean water and sanitation.

#### 7.2
- **Sub-objective:**
  - Increase the number of communities that have access to clean water and sanitation.
    - **Target:**
      - 1,500 communities by 2023.
    - **Indicators:**
      - Number of communities with access to clean water and sanitation.

#### 7.3
- **Sub-objective:**
  - Increase the number of households that have access to clean water and sanitation.
    - **Target:**
      - 1,500,000 households by 2023.
    - **Indicators:**
      - Number of households with access to clean water and sanitation.

### Details:

#### 7.1
- **Description:**
  - Development of infrastructure such as water treatment plants, reservoirs, and water supply networks for communities in rural areas.
  - Development of infrastructure and land use plans with detailed information that can be applied at the provincial level.
  - Development of comprehensive waste management systems in line with environmental standards.

#### 7.2
- **Description:**
  - The project will focus on improving access to clean water and sanitation for communities in the North-East region, including the provinces of Chiang Rai, Nan, Phayao, Lampang, Lamphun, Uttaradit, Sukhothai, Tak, and Mae Hong Son.
  - The project will also focus on improving access to clean water and sanitation for communities in the Southern region, including the provinces of Pattani, Yala, Narathiwat, Songkhla, Satun, Trang, Phatthalung, and Ranong.

#### 7.3
- **Description:**
  - The project will focus on improving access to clean water and sanitation for communities in the Central region, including the provinces of Bangkok, Nonthaburi, Pathum Thani, Nakhon Pathom, Samut Prakan, Chachoengsao, Chonburi, Rayong, Chanthaburi, Trat, Lop Buri, Saraburi, Nakhon Nayok, and Prachin Buri.
  - The project will also focus on improving access to clean water and sanitation for communities in the Northern region, including the provinces of Chiang Mai, Chiang Rai, Lampang, Lamphun, Uttaradit, Sukhothai, Tak, Mae Hong Son, Phrae, Nan, Phayao, and Phetchabun.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## Page B of 8

We range it today to learn from past uses of carbon credits to fund conservation and restoration efforts in order to conserve biodiversity and support local communities. This is part of our commitment with additional benefits to water resources, climate change mitigation, and adaptation. It includes the following components: (1) carbon sequestration through reforestation and afforestation; (2) improved forest management with quality, the lowering of illegal logging, and forest management; (3) sustainable agriculture practices that improve soil health and increase carbon sequestration; (4) water conservation by lower water use, surface water retention, and groundwater recharge; (5) sustainable fisheries and aquaculture; (6) sustainable tourism; (7) renewable energy development; (8) waste management; (9) adaptation to climate change through improved infrastructure and buildings; and (10) ESA measures. Reduction, development, and conservation, water retention, fish migration, and biodiversity.

![Figure B](image_url)

### Figure B: Example of a spatial cross-section with streams and vegetation

Include ESA measures used by proposed development are based on the variable factors of the potential ESA impact level. The project will be implemented in phases over 10 years. The first phase will include the following activities:

- **Phase I:** Conducting baseline studies, including biodiversity surveys, water quality assessments, and soil testing.
- **Phase II:** Implementing conservation measures to protect biodiversity and improve ecosystem services.

The second phase will focus on implementing sustainable practices in land use and reducing the environmental impact areas. The specific activities include:

- **Phase III:** Implementing sustainable agriculture practices that improve soil health and increase carbon sequestration.
- **Phase IV:** Implementing renewable energy projects, including solar and wind power.
- **Phase V:** Implementing water conservation measures to reduce water use and improve water quality.

The third phase will focus on implementing sustainable tourism practices that protect biodiversity and support local communities. The specific activities include:

- **Phase VI:** Developing eco-tourism guidelines for the project area.
- **Phase VII:** Implementing sustainable tourism practices, including community-based tourism initiatives.

| Agriculture   | East Coast   | Sustainable agriculture practices | Soil health improvement measures | Improve soil health by 40% | 15,000   |
| Tourism       | West Coast   | Eco-tourism development | Biodiversity conservation measures | Increase tourism revenue by 25% | 30,000   |

## B

85. Following best practices and lessons learned, local director of the ESA measures will follow a consultative approach to ensure that the project is aligned with the national climate change policy and strategy. The ESA will adopt the [GIZ's framework for mainstreaming ESA engagements on climate](https://www.giz.de/en/docs/default-source/global/knowledge-management/giz-climate-change-framework-for-mainstreaming.pdf?sfvrsn=2) as the guiding document. This framework provides a comprehensive approach to integrating climate change considerations into development cooperation projects and programs. The GIZ framework will be used to strengthen the management of water resources in the area. The consultative process will also provide an opportunity for the ESA to engage with other stakeholders, including local communities, to ensure that their needs are considered.

![Adaptation mainstreaming cycle](image-url)

**Figure 8: Adaptation mainstreaming cycle**

86. A climate-smart water management plan needs to be adapted over time to improve the framework's sustainability and effectiveness. This will involve continuous monitoring of the project's progress, including regular assessments of climate change impacts on water resources. The ESA will work with local communities to ensure that their needs are met and that they have access to clean water. The ESA will also provide training and capacity-building activities for local communities to help them adapt to climate change.

87. The ESA measures are to be integrated into a climate-smart River Basin Management approach, outlined in the [EU Water Framework Directive](https://ec.europa.eu/environment/water/water-framework/index_en.html). This will ensure that water resources are managed sustainably and that the needs of local communities are met. The ESA will also work with other stakeholders to ensure that their needs are considered.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## B

### Objective

Increase access to financing for least and most vulnerable countries. If climate vulnerability will be the first water sector (GCCA) criterion, then the Green Climate Fund (GCF) may become a major source of funding for water-related projects. The GCF is administered by a Cole Board and managed by a Secretariat based in Songdo, South Korea.

### Background

In 2013, the GCF was established to support developing countries in their efforts to address climate change. It aims to promote a paradigm shift towards low-emission and climate-resilient development pathways. The GCF is governed by 24 Board members, including representatives from developed and developing countries.

### Sub-activities Include

#### 2.1.1

- Conduct strategic environmental assessment.
- Develop vulnerability and risk assessment models.
- Assess climate change impacts on water resources.

#### 2.1.2

- Facilitate the development of climate-resilient water management strategies.
- Support the integration of climate change adaptation into national policies and plans.

#### 2.1.3

- Conduct vulnerability assessments for key water resources.
- Develop climate change scenarios based on regional models.

#### 2.1.4

- Document lessons learned from other basic (water) land planning and implementation efforts.
- Facilitate the development of climate-resilient water management strategies.

### Outputs

- Reduced vulnerability to agricultural drought in drought-prone areas through improved access to financing, investment in sustainable agriculture, and risk planning.
- Increased investment in sustainable agriculture and water management.
- Improved water security and food security for vulnerable communities.
- Enhanced capacity of local governments to manage climate risks.

### Funding Requirements

The total cost is estimated at USD 17 million. Co-financing USD 5.81 million.

Farmers emphasized the need to special support to improve their climate resilience. The farmers also requested support for capacity building on climate-smart agriculture (CSA) practices with business plans to better manage resources. To pay back, other cluster GCF will invest funds to improve infrastructure such as roads, schools, health centers, and water points. This will help the community to have access to basic services.

### Activity 3.1: Application of climate information in household agriculture planning and strengthening related support through enhanced services

The project will provide climate information and services to farmers and other users of the land to enable them to make informed decisions on their farms. The project will also provide climate information to the Ministry of Agriculture, Water and Forestry (MAWF) to improve their capacity to provide climate information to farmers. The project will also support the development of a national climate service center in Namibia.

The project will be implemented by the MAWF with technical assistance from the World Meteorological Organization (WMO). The project will be implemented over a period of five years, with an initial phase of one year followed by four years of implementation. The project will be funded by the Green Climate Fund and the Government of Namibia.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## SMART Platform

### Precision agriculture using IoT sensors, drones, and AI for sustainable farming

Precision agriculture will be employed at five sites, spanning 50 hectares (ha). IoT sensors will be installed at the farm and drones will be used for agricultural surveys. Data will be collected and analyzed to provide insights into soil health, crop growth, and weather patterns. This information will help farmers make informed decisions about planting, irrigation, and fertilization.

- **Data Collection**: Sensors and drones will collect data on soil moisture, nutrient levels, temperature, humidity, and more.
- **AI Integration**: AI algorithms will analyze the collected data to provide actionable insights for farmers.
- **Sustainability**: By optimizing resource use, precision agriculture can reduce waste and improve sustainability.

It is not expected that individual and farmers invest in IoT/sensor technologies. Rough groups of farmers may feed a shared investment pool to purchase these technologies. The Green Climate Fund (GCF) will provide financial support for the implementation of precision agriculture technology. The GCF will also support the development of an agricultural data platform, which will be used to collect and analyze data from farms that meet the green target. 30% of the total GEF will be allocated to this initiative.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## B

We urge top 50 donor countries and major international agencies to lengthen their commitment to climate finance for adaptation and mitigation. In the context of COVID-19 and the need to support a green recovery, GCF resources will also support the co-design of a climate-resilient future for farmers, preserving existing livelihoods and creating new opportunities.

### 102

To support farmers with access to finance for investments, GCF resources will also support the co-design of a credit instrument for farmers, preserving existing livelihoods and creating new opportunities. SACB has committed USD 5 million for this effort. This will be implemented in collaboration with the World Bank and other partners. The goal is to create a climate-resilient future for farmers by supporting on-farm investments that increase resilience to climate change.

### Sub-activities include:

#### 12.1

Expansion of government's climate finance policies and regulations for adaptation by farmers

#### 12.2

Support for the development of climate-smart agriculture (CSA) practices by farmers' organizations, including on-farm demonstration plots and training programs

#### 12.3

Establishment of community-based insurance schemes to protect against extreme weather events

#### 12.4

Expansion of microfinance services to support smallholder farmers' investments in climate-resilient agriculture (CSA) for long-term adaptation

#### 12.5

Eligibility criteria in climate finance policies and regulations to support farmers with adaptation investments through climate-smart agriculture (CSA)

### Activity 13: Openly holding for farmers to support market access for climate-resilient agriculture products

#### Sub-activities include:

##### 13.1

Support for the development of farmer organizations' capacity to engage in policy dialogues and advocacy on behalf of smallholder farmers

##### 13.2

Support for the development of farmer organizations' capacity to engage in policy dialogues and advocacy on behalf of smallholder farmers

### B.1: Implementation arrangements (see Table 4)

The project will be implemented by the Ministry of Agriculture, with support from the World Bank and other partners. The project will be implemented over a period of five years, with an initial phase of one year for planning and preparation.

### B.2: Monitoring and evaluation

Monitoring and evaluation arrangements will include regular reporting processes, including semi-annual review meetings to assess progress against agreed indicators. The project will also include an independent evaluation at the end of the implementation period.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal

## B

### County Programme Action Plan (CPAP) and its actions and processes outlined in the UNEP POFP Terms

The selected executing entities, after undergoing to the national implementing partners in the POFP, are required to implement the actions in accordance with the UNEP rules and procedures. The implementing partners will be guided by the approved CPAP and its actions and processes. The implementing partners will be required to submit a progress report on the implementation of the actions every six months to the National Project Coordinator (NPC) and the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP). The NPC and UNDP will also be responsible for the overall coordination, monitoring, and evaluation of the project activities.

### Proposed Organization Structure

#### Project Board
- Senior Beneficiaries (ONMR, CMR, IGAD, RDO, LDC, etc.)
  - Executive Director General of RDO (Chair)
  - Senior Representative UNDP
  - Senior Representative UNEP

#### Project Administration Unit (PAU)
- National Project Director
- Technical Advisory Group
  - Environment, water, agriculture

#### Project Support (PSU)
- Administrative and Financial Coordinator
- Communication, Field Coordinator
- Procurement Officer
- Legal and Admin Officer

## B

### 103
The Project Manager is responsible for making sure that administrative, technical, financial decisions are made in a transparent and accountable manner. The Project Manager will ensure that all project activities are implemented according to the approved work plan and budget. The Project Manager will also be responsible for the overall management of the project, including the development of public, social, economic, environmental, and gender-sensitive components. The Project Manager will report directly to the National Programme Officer (NPO) on a regular basis.

### 104
Human Resources will ensure that all project management requirements for hiring, compensation, benefits, and other human resources issues are met in accordance with UNDP rules and regulations. Human Resources will also be responsible for ensuring compliance with all relevant laws and regulations related to employment. The Human Resources department will report directly to the National Programme Officer (NPO).

- An Executive Team approved by National Implementing Partner that holds the project ownership and accountability.
- A Senior Supervisor responsible for providing guidance regarding the technical feasibility of the projects.
- A Project Manager who is responsible for the day-to-day management of the project, including financial oversight and reporting to UNDP.
- A Technical Specialist (UNV) appointed by UNDP to capacity as GEF Accredited Entity.
- A Senior Technical Advisor responsible for providing technical advice on project benefits from the perspective of project beneficiaries.

### 110
The National Programme Officer is a senior representative from RIO who is responsible for the overall direction and management of the project. The NPO will be responsible for ensuring that all project activities are implemented in accordance with UNDP rules and regulations, as well as the approved work plan and budget.

### 112
The Project Manager will not be paid by a donor but rather by the UNDP. The Project Manager will be accountable to the National Programme Officer (NPO) for the overall management of the project. The Project Manager is responsible for ensuring that all project activities are implemented in accordance with the approved work plan and budget, as well as the project procedures, the quality standards, and the reporting requirements.

### 113
UNDP will transfer funds to the Royal Government of Cambodia (RGC) under the Ministry of Agriculture and Forestry. The RGC will be responsible for managing a project that is implemented by a local NGO. The RGC will also be responsible for ensuring that all project activities are implemented in accordance with UNDP rules and regulations, as well as the approved work plan and budget.

The Human Resources department will be responsible for the recruitment of personnel and staff. The Human Resources department will also be responsible for ensuring compliance with all relevant laws and regulations related to employment. The Human Resources department will report directly to the National Programme Officer (NPO).

### 114
The project will be implemented by a local NGO that is registered in Cambodia. The NGO will be responsible for implementing the project activities, as well as ensuring compliance with all relevant laws and regulations related to employment.

### 115
The project will be funded by UNDP under the GEF Small Grants Programme (SGP). The SGP is a global programme that provides funding to local communities for small-scale development projects. The SGP is implemented in more than 120 countries around the world.

**Parallel Finance**

UNEP did not use any subsidies specific to the field of climate change with the GCF. The only exception is the use of a small amount of funding from the Global Environment Facility (GEF) for the development of the UNEP-GCF website.

A UNEP internal assessment was conducted for R&D as implementing partner entities, which all major projects are required to undertake. This assessment is carried out annually and is based on the [Responsible Parties for Outputs](https://www.unep.org) and [Output 2 Activity 22](https://www.unep.org). Please see Annex 2 for further details.

UNEP provides a three-year oversight and quality assurance risk including UNEP staff in Country Offices and Headquarters. This risk is shared with the implementing partner entities, which are required to undertake an annual self-assessment of their own internal systems. The risk management system is based on the [UNEP Environmental Management System](https://www.unep.org) (UEMS). As an Accredited Entity to the GCF, UNEP’s oversight project design and implementation is subject to the GCF’s oversight project design and implementation.

S.1. A justification for GCF funding requires three words, demonstrating 2 digits:

- The first digit is the number of words in the justification.
- The second digit is the number of characters in the justification.
- The third digit is the number of sentences in the justification.

Please see Annex 3 for further details.

Alternative financing was addressed simultaneously to change the most suitable terms as well as to ensure that the project is implemented in a timely manner. This includes the use of alternative funding sources, such as grants or loans, to cover the costs of the project. Please see Annex 4 for further details.

## PAGE 1 OF 8

water management interventions for farmers and agribusiness to enhance business skills for processing market access and value addition. The project will also promote climate smart agriculture practices that increase agricultural productivity, improve water use efficiency, and reduce greenhouse gas emissions from agriculture and livestock. The project will contribute towards achieving the national development goals of enhancing food security, improving livelihoods, and increasing resilience to climate change.

### 118

The project will be implemented by the Government of Uganda (GoU) through the Ministry of Water and Environment (MWE) in collaboration with the National Agricultural Research Organization (NARO). The project will also engage the private sector and other stakeholders such as farmer organizations, local governments, and communities. The project will be implemented at the national level and in selected districts.

### 119

An integrated project such as this one, however, can only enter into the planning and design stage after a detailed feasibility study has been conducted to assess the climate change vulnerabilities of the target areas and to identify the most appropriate interventions. This study will also provide information on the availability of water resources for irrigation and other uses, the potential for value addition, and the economic viability of the proposed interventions.

### 120

The project will be implemented in two phases. The first phase will involve a feasibility study and the development of a detailed project proposal. The second phase will involve the implementation of the project. The project is expected to be completed within five years.

### 121

The project will be funded by the Green Climate Fund (GCF) through the Special Climate Change Fund (SCCF). The project will also receive co-financing from the Government of Uganda and other stakeholders. The total cost of the project is estimated to be US$50 million, with the GCF providing US$30 million and the Government of Uganda providing the remaining US$20 million.

### 122

The project will be implemented in collaboration with the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP) and other international organizations. The UNDP will provide technical assistance and capacity building support to the Government of Uganda and other stakeholders. The project will also benefit from the expertise of the World Bank, the International Fund for Agricultural Development (IFAD), and other international organizations.

### 123

The project will be implemented in accordance with the principles of gender equality, environmental sustainability, and social inclusion. The project will also promote the use of climate-smart technologies and practices that are appropriate to the local context and that can be easily adopted by farmers and agribusinesses.

### 124

The project will be monitored and evaluated using a results-based management approach. The project will also be subject to an independent evaluation at the mid-term and final stages of implementation. The project will also be subject to an ex-post evaluation to assess the long-term impacts of the project.

### 120
The proposed project has been designed in close consultation with and inclusion of relevant government agencies in the target-based areas. These consultations and inclusive processes are conducted by local project authorities who are well versed with the socio-economic, cultural, environmental, and political contexts as outlined in the Proposal Report (Annex A). Building on this foundation, the project ensures that the development objectives are aligned with national priorities and strategies for climate change adaptation and mitigation.

### 120.1
The project will strengthen existing systems to ensure effective monitoring, evaluation, and reporting of project activities. This effort will enhance existing systems by integrating better data collection and more robust indicators (including proxy indicators) to measure progress towards the project's objectives. The project will also improve the capacity of local authorities to monitor and evaluate climate change adaptation and mitigation efforts.

### 120.2
The project will establish an environmental management system, GMES, which will be used to track the implementation of project activities. This system will provide a platform for better integration with other RIO and RETG systems. ### f) Description of financing needs

The project seeks USD 17,344 million of GCF support with a total co-financing of USD 18,384 million which will be used to finance the construction and operation of the Green Climate Fund (GCF). The GCF will provide funding for climate change mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries. The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 25% by 2030 and increase resilience to climate impacts by 20%. The project is expected to be completed within five years.

### g) Co-financing arrangements

GCF funds co-financing as well as grants to raise 3 percent of the total amount. This will be used to develop a sustainable financing strategy for the project, which includes the development of a financial plan and risk management framework. The project is expected to be completed within five years.

### h) Contribution to NDCs

The co-financing from RTF, through GFD, will be a budget allocation which RD will allocate for the project. This will be used to finance the construction and operation of the Green Climate Fund (GCF). The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 25% by 2030 and increase resilience to climate impacts by 20%. The project is expected to be completed within five years.

## 1. Impact generated (net SDG value, environmental and social impacts)

- Increased knowledge and enhanced capabilities of the local communities through training and capacity building.
- Improved livelihoods of 600 households through increased income from sustainable agriculture and forestry.
- Enhanced food security and diet quality for women and youth through improved nutrition and food safety for food-insecure families to increase their resilience to climate change.
- Increased access to clean water, sanitation, and hygiene facilities by reducing open defecation and improving water management practices.
- Improved health outcomes of women and children through better access to healthcare services, including reproductive health services.
- Enhanced capacity of local governments to implement and monitor climate-smart agriculture practices.

## D

### TB

The proposed project integrates sustainable livelihoods and practices for replication and scale to climate resilient agriculture and forestry systems. It will enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities through improved water management and technologies, sustainable land use planning, ecosystem-based adaptation (EBA) in water management, and integrated watershed management. EBA implementation will be based on local knowledge and traditional practices, which are often linked to biodiversity conservation and sustainable resource use.

### 138

In particular, the enabling activities choose the potential for transformative impact beyond the project and scale a portfolio of activities that can be replicated and scaled up.

The project also establishes a legal framework and capacity building. The project will end RPL and its implementation will be based on the RPL law, which will support local government to implement the project. It will provide a comprehensive approach to climate change adaptation and mitigation through integrated watershed management, sustainable land use planning, ecosystem-based adaptation (EBA) in water management, and integrated watershed management.

### Climate RPL's objectives

Climate RPL's objectives is integrate expertise-based adaptation (EBA) in water management. Where EBA is implemented, it will be based on local knowledge and traditional practices that are often linked to biodiversity conservation and sustainable resource use. The project will also support the development of the benefits derived from the ecosystem services in light of increased climate risks.

### The project sets out the process of identifying local level agriculture community vulnerabilities to climate change

The project sets out the process of identifying local level agriculture community vulnerabilities to climate change. It will provide a comprehensive approach to climate change adaptation and mitigation through integrated watershed management, sustainable land use planning, ecosystem-based adaptation (EBA) in water management, and integrated watershed management.

### Integration of finance

Integration of finance with other actors such as Microfinance Bank and SMEC, and implementation of the project will be based on local knowledge and traditional practices that are often linked to biodiversity conservation and sustainable resource use. The project will also support the development of the benefits derived from the ecosystem services in light of increased climate risks.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v2.1 | PAGE 4 OF 8

Request for funds to advance the institutional framework for adaptation, long-term planning for climate change management, and promote a strong enabling environment for the private sector.

## 150
The proposed GCF project is aligned with national priorities and builds on existing government efforts to enhance climate resilience. It also aligns with the broader academic research agenda to integrate climate change into governance structures and policy frameworks.

## 151
Pioneer's Technology Needs Assessment (TNA) report formulated in 2019 identified three major impact areas of climate change:
- **Water Resource Management**: In need of networking (both local and central) and management of water resources.
- **Agriculture**: Requires improved technology, irrigation systems, and soil conservation techniques to enhance agricultural research, higher research and teaching (HRT), rural development, and an integrated management system.
- **Health**: Needs a strong health system with a focus on climate change adaptation.

## 152
Addressing the need to strengthen sustainable water resource management, the 12th National Economic and Social Development Plan (NESDP) (2016–2020) emphasizes:
- The development of water resources.
- The promotion of water conservation among farmers for improved water plans and efficiency.
- Enhancing water resource information centers, and
- Establishing a national water management organization at the local level, such as river basin organizations.

## 153
Reflecting a priority in Thailand's adaptation strategy, which is guided by the philosophy of Sufficiency Economy, the project aims to enhance resilience through:
- Improved irrigation systems.
- Integrated water resource management.
- The New Theory for Sustainable Development (NTDSD) approach.
- The National Economic and Social Development Plan (2017–2021) has proposed a budget to support the development of climate change adaptation measures.

## 154
The project will be implemented in collaboration with:
- **Ministry of Agriculture and Cooperatives**: Aims to enhance agricultural productivity, improve water management systems, and promote sustainable farming practices.
- **Ministry of Natural Resources and Environment (MONRE)**: Focuses on climate change adaptation and mitigation strategies.

## 155
Development of Thailand's Climate Change National Adaptation Plan (NAP) formulated in 2019 and currently under the Department of Environmental Quality Promotion (DEQP), Ministry of Natural Resources and Environment (MONRE). The NAP is aligned with the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) and the Paris Agreement.

## 156
The National Disaster Warning System (NDWS) for Thailand includes a sharing of adaptation key efforts.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 4 OF 8

## D

### Integrating UNDP’s MDGs, Paris Agreement objectives, and other relevant international agreements (e.g., CBD, UNCCD) and other global initiatives (e.g., Bonn Challenge, REDD+)

### 164.
The proposal is aligned with UNDP's comprehensive approach in the areas of capacity building, poverty reduction, and environmental sustainability. It aims to enhance the livelihoods of local communities through sustainable land management practices. These include community-based afforestation, deforestation control, soil and water conservation, watershed management, and development of climate-smart agricultural practices. The project will be implemented in collaboration with local governments, non-governmental organizations (NGOs), and other stakeholders to ensure sustainability and long-term benefits. It is aligned with the UNDP's strategic objectives and the broader goals of the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP) and the Millennium Development Goals (MDGs).

### 165.
Efforts and effectiveness across five years, programmed in eight phases:
- **Phase I:** Capacity building for local communities on sustainable land management practices.
- **Phases II-IV:** Implementation of sustainable land management practices at the community level.
- **Phase V:** Monitoring and evaluation of project outcomes.
- **Phase VI:** Documentation and dissemination of lessons learned.
- **Phase VII:** Scaling up successful practices to other communities.
- **Phase VIII:** Long-term sustainability planning.

### 166.
Economic costs (direct and indirect) are all measured in real terms of 2018. Economic costs of the project are estimated at USD 5 million over five years, with an average annual expenditure of USD 1 million. The economic benefits are expected to be significant, including increased agricultural productivity, improved water security, and enhanced ecosystem services.

### 167.
The project aims to enhance the economic benefits by ensuring that it can benefit more than 20,000 households directly involved in sustainable land management practices over five years. It is estimated that the 20,000 households will see an average increase of USD 500 per household annually.

### 168.
As a country with varying weather patterns and economic challenges, sustainable land management practices are essential to ensure long-term resilience and adaptation. These efforts were also able to be done in areas where traditional agricultural practices were not sufficient to meet the needs of the community.

### 169.
The project identifies ten important benefits of a different nature: Fast climate change (mitigated changes in precipitation patterns), improved soil fertility, increased agricultural productivity, enhanced water security, improved livelihoods, reduced poverty levels, improved food security, enhanced biodiversity, improved ecosystem services, and an overall increase in the resilience of communities.

### 170.
The present value (NPV) and internal rate of return (IRR) of the proposed investment project are presented below. The NPV is USD 2 million, and the IRR is 15%. This indicates that the project is financially viable and will generate significant economic benefits over its lifetime.

### 17.1
A sensitivity analysis indicates that the NPV of the project is subject to an increase cost of 20%, a decrease in benefits of 20%, and an inflation increase of 5%. These factors would result in a negative NPV, indicating that the project may not be financially viable under these conditions.

### 17.2
The analysis further indicates that the project is sensitive to changes in discount rates. A higher discount rate would reduce the present value of future cash flows, making the project less attractive from an investment perspective.

### 17.3
All three are included and activities that constitute this proposed project. However, none of the activities or activities already exist and have been implemented by the Government. Therefore, the project is new and has not been implemented before. The project will be implemented in the next five years.

### 17.4
Environment: According to the EPA's environmental assessment rules, the project proposed activities have been analyzed from a macro-economic and governmental perspective to assess the impact of GCF grants as they may boost economic growth and reduce poverty levels. The project is expected to have minimal adverse environmental impacts and will be implemented in accordance with environmental regulations.

### 17.5
With 60% of budget coming from co-financing, GCF grant requested through the funding proposal is USD 3 million. This amount has been calculated based on the cost of activities that are subject to the environment, social, and gender policies. The project will be implemented in accordance with the environmental and social safeguards policies.

### 17.6
A sensitivity analysis indicates that the NPV of the project is subject to an increase cost of 20%, a decrease in benefits of 20%, and an inflation increase of 5%. These factors would result in a negative NPV, indicating that the project may not be financially viable under these conditions.

### 17.7
To ensure sustainability of the investment, the TF has committed USD 17 million for 15 years funded by GCF funded project.

### 17.8
The project feasibility study was included as part of development activities for the proposed GCF project. Candidate communities were selected based on their vulnerability to climate change and their capacity to implement the project.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## Objective

Nature-based and farming solutions to the climate, ultimate, and other farmers against climate change.

### 128

The proposed measures in this project will support more effective water management and are necessary to improve the resilience of agricultural systems. The project will be implemented through a combination of activities including:

- **Restoration of degraded lands** with the support of local communities.
- **Improvement of soil health** by promoting sustainable land use practices.
- **Activities that reduce the warming of local water bodies**.
- **Promotion of climate-smart agriculture**.

### 129

The project will address climate change by restoring and protecting degraded lands. The project will also support the development of a robust monitoring system to track progress towards achieving its objectives.

## Activities

- **Restoration of degraded lands** including reforestation, afforestation, and agroforestry.
- **Improvement of soil health** through sustainable land use practices.
- **Promotion of climate-smart agriculture**.
- **Monitoring and evaluation** of project activities to ensure they are achieving their intended outcomes.

## Expected Outcomes

- **Restoration of degraded lands** leading to improved biodiversity and ecosystem services.
- **Improved soil health** resulting in increased agricultural productivity.
- **Reduced greenhouse gas emissions** from agriculture.
- **Increased resilience** of farming communities to climate change.

### 130

The project will also support the development of a robust monitoring system to track progress towards achieving its objectives. This will include:

- **Regular reporting on project activities**.
- **Monitoring of greenhouse gas emissions**.
- **Assessment of the economic viability of project activities**.

## Agricultural Extension and Community-Based Resource Management

Agricultural extension and community-based resource management are critical for climate-smart agriculture and sustainable development in the New and New River basins. The project will support the establishment of climate-smart agriculture practices, including:

- **Training for farmers** on climate-smart agriculture.
- **Promotion of sustainable land use practices**.
- **Establishment of farmer-to-farmer networks**.

### 131

The project will also support the development of a robust monitoring system to track progress towards achieving its objectives. This will include:

- **Regular reporting on project activities**.
- **Monitoring of greenhouse gas emissions**.
- **Assessment of the economic viability of project activities**.

## Climate-Smart Agricultural Practices

Climate-smart agricultural practices are essential for improving the resilience of farming communities in the New and New River basins. The project will support the adoption of climate-smart agricultural practices, including:

- **Promotion of conservation agriculture**.
- **Introduction of drought-tolerant crop varieties**.
- **Improvement of water management practices**.

### 132

The project will also support the development of a robust monitoring system to track progress towards achieving its objectives. This will include:

- **Regular reporting on project activities**.
- **Monitoring of greenhouse gas emissions**.
- **Assessment of the economic viability of project activities**.

## Health Impacts and Benefits for Local Communities

Health impacts and benefits for local communities are an important aspect of the project. The project will support health interventions to reduce the impact of climate change on human health, including:

- **Promotion of healthy diets** to improve nutrition.
- **Improvement of water quality**.
- **Reduction of vector-borne diseases**.

### 133

The project will also support the development of a robust monitoring system to track progress towards achieving its objectives. This will include:

- **Regular reporting on project activities**.
- **Monitoring of greenhouse gas emissions**.
- **Assessment of the economic viability of project activities**.

## Water Management in Areas of Potential Conflicts

### 162.
The project management unit will monitor water supplies for the project area in Tung Bang Ratthan and Tung Bang Ratthan Nok. The monitoring will be conducted by the local authorities at a central point, between the streams of Ouai and the new canal. The monitoring will increase the water supply but also reduce the amount of water that flows into the stream. This will affect the volume of water flowing in accordance with the cutting of canals.
